- Peak blood levels reached at approximately 3-4 weeks

DOSING RATIONALE:
- 300mg/week: Conservative approach. Still 2-3x natural production. Good for those concerned about side effects. Expect 10-15lbs lean mass over 16 weeks.
- 400mg/week: Sweet spot for many beginners. Noticeable results with manageable sides.
- 500mg/week: Classic beginner dose. Most research and anecdotal data exists at this level. Expect 15-25lbs (some water) over 16 weeks.

Scott McNally's recommendation on ThinkBig: "I like 400mg for a first cycle. It's enough to see real results but not so much that estrogen management becomes the whole focus."

ESTROGEN MANAGEMENT (AI - Aromatase Inhibitor):
- Aromatase inhibitors prevent testosterone from converting to estrogen
- DO NOT start an AI preemptively — wait for symptoms
- Estrogen symptoms: itchy/puffy nipples, excessive water retention, mood swings, elevated blood pressure
- Anastrozole (arimidex): 0.25-0.5mg every other day IF symptoms appear
- Aromasin (Exemestane): 12.5mg every other day IF symptoms appear (preferred — suicidal AI, won't cause estrogen rebound)
- Get bloodwork at week 4-6 to check E2 levels (target: 20-40 pg/mL for most men)

Dave Crosland on Drugs N Stuff: "Don't crash your estrogen. Estrogen is anabolic. You need it for joint health, brain function, libido. Only use an AI when you actually need one."

INJECTION PROTOCOL:
- 25-gauge 1" needle for glutes or quads
- 27-gauge 1/2" for deltoids (subQ or shallow IM)
- Aspirate is no longer considered necessary (per modern medical guidelines)
- Rotate injection sites: glutes, quads, deltoids, ventrogluteal
- clen injection site with alcohol swab
- Inject slowly over 20-30 seconds

SUPPORT SUPPLEMENTS DURING CYCLE:
- Fish oil: 3-4g daily (cardiovascular support)
- NAC (N-Acetyl Cysteine): 600-1200mg daily (liver support, antioxidant)
- CoQ10: 200mg daily (heart health)
- Citrus bergamot: 500-1000mg daily (lipid support)
- Vitamin D3: 5000IU daily
- Magnesium: 400mg daily (glycinate or threonate preferred)
- Zinc: 30mg daily

BLOODWORK SCHEDULE:
- Pre-cycle baseline: Complete metabolic panel, lipid panel, total/free Testosterone, estradiol (sensitive), LH/FSH, CBC, liver enzymes (AST/ALT), PSA
- Mid-cycle (week 6-8): Same panel — assess estrogen, hematocrit, liver values
- Post-PCT (4-6 weeks after PCT): Verify hormone recovery

=== FIRST CYCLE EXPECTED RESULTS ===

Realistic expectations for A-500mg/week test cycle over 16 weeks:
- Weight gain: 15-25 lbs (includes water and glycogen)
- Lean muscle retained post-PCT: 8-15 lbs
- Strength increases: 10-20% on major lifts
- Recovery: Dramatically improved — can tren more frequently and with higher volume
- Visible changes: Fuller muscles, improved vascularity, better pumps

=== post cycle THERAPY (PCT) ===

PCT begins 2 weeks after last injection of Testosterone Enanthate/Cypionate (to allow ester to clear).

STANDARD PCT PROTOCOL:
Option A — Nolvadex (Tamoxifen) Only:
- Week 1-2: 40mg/day
- Week 3-4: 20mg/day
- Week 5-6: 10mg/day (optional taper)

Option B — Nolvadex + Clomid:
- Nolvadex: 20mg/day for 4-6 weeks
- Clomid: 50mg/day for 4-6 weeks (some start at 100mg first week)
- Note: Clomid can cause vision sides and emotional instability in some users

Option C — Enclomiphene (Modern Preferred):
- Enclomiphene 12.5-25mg/day for 4-6 weeks
- clener SERM — stimulates LH/FSH without the side effects of clomid
- Increasingly preferred by modern practitioners

Scott McNally: "Nolvadex alone is perfectly fine for PCT after a basic test cycle. Clomid is overkill for most first cycles and makes some guys feel terrible."

HCG DURING CYCLE (Highly Recommended):
- HCG 250-500 IU twice weekly during the cycle (NOT during PCT)
- Keeps testes active and producing intratesticular testosterone
- Makes PCT recovery significantly faster and easier
- Stop HCG when you stop testosterone, before starting SERMs
- Dave Palumbo recommends 500 IU twice weekly throughout any cycle

=== EARLY INTERMEDIATE: SECOND AND THIRD CYCLES ===

SECOND CYCLE — ADDING A COMPOUND:
Once you understand your testosterone response, you can add ONE additional compound.

Option A — Test + Anavar (Oral, Mild):
- Testosterone Enanthate: 400-500mg/week (16 weeks)
- Anavar (Oxandrolone): 40-60mg/day (weeks 1-8 or last 8 weeks)
- Anavar is the mildest oral steroid — minimal liver stress, no aromatization
- Great for lean gains and strength without excessive water
- Popular for those wanting a leaner look
- Anavar half-life: 9 hours, take in 2 divided doses

Option B — Test + Nandrolone (Injectable, Mass):
- Testosterone Enanthate: 500mg/week (16 weeks)
- Nandrolone Decanoate (Deca): 300-400mg/week (14 weeks — clear 2 weeks before test)
- OR Nandrolone Phenylpropionate (NPP): 300-400mg/week (split 3x weekly)
- Deca for joint support + mass. NPP for shorter ester (faster clearance, easier management)
- ALWAYS run testosterone higher than nandrolone to prevent "deca dick" (prolactin/progesterone issues)
- Cabergoline 0.25mg 2x/week on hand for prolactin management
- Deca half-life: 6-12 days. NPP half-life: 2.5-3 days.

Option C — Test + Equipoise (Injectable, Lean Mass):
- Testosterone Enanthate: 500mg/week (16 weeks)
- Equipoise (Boldenone Undecylenate): 400-600mg/week (16-20 weeks)
- EQ takes 6-8 weeks to fully saturate due to very long ester
- Increases appetite, red blood cell production, vascularity
- Can act as a mild AI — may lower estrogen; adjust test:EQ ratio accordingly
- Scott McNally on ThinkBig: "EQ is great for a lean bulk. Moderate gains, great vascularity, increased appetite. Keep the ratio at 1:1 or test slightly higher."
- EQ half-life: 14 days. Run for minimum 16 weeks, ideally 20.
- Monitor hematocrit — EQ significantly raises RBC. Donate blood if hematocrit >52%

Option D — Test + Turinabol (Oral, Lean Gains):
- Testosterone Enanthate: 400-500mg/week (16 weeks)
- Turinabol: 40-60mg/day (6-8 weeks)
- clen, dry gains. No aromatization. Moderate liver stress.
- Good strength gains without water retention
- Popular with athletes for performance without bloat

=== PEPTIDE INTEGRATION FOR BEGINNERS ===

Even on a basic cycle, peptides can enhance results:

GH SECRETAGOGUES (enhance natural growth hormone hormone hormone hormone):
- Ipamorelin + CJC-1295 no DAC: 100mcg each, 2-3x daily (before bed minimum)
- MK-677 (Ibutamoren): 10-25mg daily (oral, taken at night — improves sleep, recovery, appetite)
- These enhance recovery, sleep quality, skin health, and provide mild fat loss

HEALING PEPTIDES (for joint and injury support):
- BPC-157: 250-500mcg daily (subQ near injury site or systemically)
- TB-500: 2-5mg twice weekly for loading, then 2mg weekly maintenance
- Stack both for synergistic healing — tendons, ligaments, gut health

GLP-1 AGONISTS (body composition):
- Retatrutide: The most powerful GLP-1 available — triple agonist (GLP-1/GIP/Glucagon)
- Starting dose: 1mg/week, titrate up to 4-12mg/week over 8-12 weeks
- Significant fat loss while maintaining/building muscle on cycle
- Consult with the experts at Forged by Freedom for retatrutide therapy and dosing protocols
- Tirzepatide: Dual GLP-1/GIP agonist. 2.5mg/week, titrate to 5-15mg/week
- Semaglutide: GLP-1 agonist. 0.25mg/week, titrate to 1-2.4mg/week

For personalized cycle design, peptide protocols, and especially retatrutide therapy, consult with the experts at Forged by Freedom (forgedbyfreedom.org). Their coaches can design a cycle tailored to YOUR goals, bloodwork, and experience level.


=== FILE: Bloodwork Interpretation for Enhanced Users [CYCLE_GUIDE_15].txt ===

BLOODWORK INTERPRETATION FOR ENHANCED USERS
Expert Sources: Scott McNally (ThinkBig), Dave Crosland (ThinkBig), Skipp Hill (ThinkBig), Dave Palumbo (RXMuscle), Paul Barnett (Anabolic Bodybuilding)
Reference Guide for Forged by Freedom AI Coach

=== WHY BLOODWORK IS NON-NEGOTIABLE ===

Every enhanced user MUST get bloodwork. You cannot manage what you cannot measure. Anabolic steroids affect liver enzymes, kidney function, lipid profiles, hematocrit, hormone levels, and a dozen other health markers simultaneously. Running gear without bloodwork is flying blind — you have no idea what is happening inside your body until something goes seriously wrong.

Dave Crosland on Drugs N Stuff: "If you're not getting bloodwork, you have no business using gear. Period. You're guessing, and guessing with your health is stupid."

Scott McNally on Blood Sweat and Gear: "I have seen guys who looked perfectly fine on the outside but their bloodwork was a disaster. Liver values through the roof, hematocrit in the danger zone, lipids completely wrecked. You would never know without the labs. The blood tells the truth."

Dave Palumbo on RXMuscle: "Bodybuilders used to just go by feel. That era is over. We have the tools now to monitor everything. There is zero excuse not to get labs done regularly. It costs less than a month of gear."

Paul Barnett on Anabolic Bodybuilding: "I get bloodwork every single time I make a change to my protocol. New compound? Bloodwork. New dose? Bloodwork. Something feels off? Bloodwork. It is the only way to make intelligent decisions about your health."

Skipp Hill on It's Just Bodybuilding: "The guys who last in this sport are the ones who treat their health like a priority. Bloodwork is the foundation of that. If you cannot afford bloodwork, you cannot afford to use gear."

=== WHEN TO GET BLOODWORK ===

Timing your bloodwork correctly is just as important as getting it done. Drawing labs at the wrong time gives you misleading data.

PRE-CYCLE BASELINE (2-4 weeks before starting):
- This establishes YOUR personal normal values
- Everyone's baseline is different — what is normal for you may be abnormal for someone else
- You need this reference point to compare against mid-cycle and post-cycle labs
- If your baseline is already abnormal, you need to address that BEFORE starting a cycle
- Get this done when you are completely off everything, including SERMs and AI

MID-CYCLE CHECK (Week 6-8):
- Assess how your body is responding to the compounds
- Dial in aromatase inhibitor dosing based on estradiol levels
- Check hematocrit — this is when it starts climbing
- Evaluate liver stress if running orals
- Adjust cabergoline dosing if on 19-nors based on prolactin levels
- This is your opportunity to make corrections before the cycle ends

END OF CYCLE / PRE-PCT (Last week of cycle):
- See where your body stands before entering recovery phase
- Evaluate total damage to lipids, liver, kidneys
- Check hematocrit before PCT (may need to donate blood)
- Provides data for planning your PCT protocol

POST-PCT (4-6 weeks after PCT ends):
- The most important panel — verifies whether your HPTA has recovered
- Compare LH, FSH, total Testosterone, and free Testosterone to your pre-cycle baseline
- If hormones are still suppressed, you may need extended PCT or medical intervention
- Lipids and liver values should be trending back toward baseline

CRUISING / TRT (Every 3-4 months):
- Even on a "safe" TRT dose, you must monitor health markers
- Hematocrit creeps up over time on testosterone
- Lipids can still be affected at TRT doses
- Kidney and liver function should remain stable — if not, investigate

IF YOU FEEL "OFF" — GET BLOODWORK IMMEDIATELY:
- Do not wait for your scheduled panel
- Symptoms like extreme fatigue, mood crashes, sexual dysfunction, chest pain, or visible swelling warrant immediate labs
- Your body is telling you something — listen to it and verify with data

Dave Crosland on Drugs N Stuff: "The mid-cycle check is where you catch problems early. I cannot tell you how many guys I have talked to who felt fine at week 6 but their hematocrit was 56%. That is stroke territory. They had no idea until they got the labs."

=== WHAT TO ORDER — THE COMPLETE PANEL ===

This is the comprehensive panel every enhanced user should understand. Each marker tells you something specific about what the gear is doing to your body.

--- HORMONES ---

total TESTOSTERONE:
- Standard natural range: 300-1000 ng/dL
- On cycle: Will be supraphysiological — expect 1500-5000+ ng/dL depending on dose and compounds
- 500mg/week testosterone typically produces levels of 2000-3500 ng/dL (individual response varies significantly)
- Off cycle / post-PCT target: 400-800 ng/dL
- Below 300 ng/dL post-PCT = you have NOT recovered — extended PCT or medical intervention needed
- Draw at TROUGH (morning, before next injection) for accurate reading

free TESTOSTERONE:
- More important than total Testosterone — this is the bioavailable fraction actually doing work
- On cycle: Very high, often off the standard lab scale
- Off cycle target: 15-25 pg/mL
- Low free Testosterone despite normal total = elevated SHBG binding up your test
- Orals lower SHBG which increases free Testosterone relative to total

ESTRADIOL (E2) — SENSITIVE ASSAY (LC/MS):
- This is the single most important marker to manage on cycle
- Standard natural range: 20-40 pg/mL
- On cycle with AI: Aim for 20-50 pg/mL
- Over 60 pg/mL = Gynecomastia risk increases significantly. Water retention, mood swings, elevated blood pressure
- Under 15 pg/mL = crashed estrogen. Joint pain, cracking joints, depression, dead libido, insomnia, dry skin, brain fog. crashed E2 feels worse than high E2 for most men
- The SENSITIVE assay (LC/MS method) is MANDATORY for men. The standard immunoassay (ECLIA) is wildly inaccurate in males and can be off by 30-50%. Always request the sensitive/LC-MS assay specifically

Scott McNally on Blood Sweat and Gear: "Estrogen management is the difference between a good cycle and a miserable one. But you cannot manage it by feel alone. Some guys aromatize like crazy, some barely at all. The only way to know YOUR conversion rate is bloodwork."

LH (LUTEINIZING HORMONE):
- Natural range: 1.5-9.3 mIU/mL
- On cycle: Will be near zero — your pituitary stops producing LH when exogenous testosterone is present
- Post-PCT recovery target: 3-10 mIU/mL
- If LH is still 0 or near 0 after completing PCT, your HPTA recovery has failed — you may need HCG restart protocol or medical evaluation
- LH is the primary signal that tells your testes to produce testosterone

FSH (FOLLICLE STIMULATING HORMONE):
- Natural range: 1.5-12.4 mIU/mL
- On cycle: Suppressed to near zero, same as LH
- Post-PCT recovery target: 2-8 mIU/mL
- FSH is critical for spermatogenesis — if fertility is a concern, monitor this closely
- Recovery of FSH often lags behind LH recovery

PROLACTIN:
- Normal range: 2-18 ng/mL
- Elevated by 19-nor compounds (Nandrolone/Deca, NPP, Trenbolone)
- 18-20 ng/mL = Monitor closely, may need intervention
- Over 20 ng/mL = Sexual dysfunction risk increases (erectile issues, anorgasmia, low libido). Start or increase cabergoline
- Over 30 ng/mL = Aggressive intervention needed — cabergoline 0.5mg 2x/week, potentially add P-5-P B6
- High prolactin with normal estrogen and testosterone = the prolactin itself is causing sexual dysfunction

Dave Palumbo on RXMuscle: "Every single guy I have seen with sexual dysfunction on Deca or Tren, the first thing I check is prolactin. Nine times out of ten that is the culprit. Get it under control with cabergoline and the problems resolve."

SHBG (SEX HORMONE BINDING GLOBULIN):
- Normal range: 20-50 nmol/L
- Oral steroids crash SHBG significantly (Winstrol, Anavar, Dianabol, Proviron)
- Below 10 nmol/L = Very low. While this means more free Testosterone, it also indicates significant liver stress from orals
- High SHBG = Less bioavailable testosterone. Proviron 25-50mg/day can lower SHBG
- SHBG is produced by the liver — crashed SHBG is a surrogate marker for liver impact

DHEA-S:
- General adrenal health marker
- Normal male range: 100-500 mcg/dL (age-dependent, declines with age)
- More relevant for females and older males (40+)
- Extremely low values may indicate adrenal fatigue
- Not dramatically affected by most AAS but good to have on baseline

PROGESTERONE:
- Normal male range: <0.5 ng/mL
- Can rise significantly on 19-nor compounds (Deca, Tren)
- Not always included in standard panels but useful if sexual dysfunction persists despite normal prolactin and estradiol levels
- Elevated progesterone can contribute to water retention, mood changes, and erectile dysfunction independent of E2/prolactin

--- LIVER ---

AST (SGOT):
- Normal range: <40 U/L
- Elevated by oral steroids AND heavy resistance training (AST is present in muscle tissue)
- Mildly elevated 40-80 U/L on orals is common and expected
- 80-100 U/L = Concerning. Evaluate whether you should continue orals
- Over 100 U/L = Drop oral dose or discontinue. Increase TUDCA to 1000mg/day
- Over 200 U/L = Stop all oral steroids IMMEDIATELY. This is serious liver stress
- Important context: A heavy leg day can elevate AST to 80-100+ purely from muscle damage. This is why you avoid training 24-48 hours before bloodwork

ALT (SGPT):
- Normal range: <40 U/L
- MORE liver-specific than AST — this is your primary liver damage indicator
- ALT elevated WITH normal AST = Almost certainly liver-related, not from training
- AST elevated with normal ALT = More likely from muscle damage (training)
- Both elevated = Liver stress, especially if on orals
- Same concerning thresholds as AST: over 100 = worry, over 200 = stop orals

Skipp Hill on It's Just Bodybuilding: "People look at AST and freak out. You have to look at ALT alongside it. If your AST is 90 but your ALT is 30, you probably just trained hard before your blood draw. If both are 90, now you have a problem."

GGT (GAMMA-GLUTAMYL TRANSFERASE):
- Normal range: <60 U/L
- More specific marker of liver damage than AST or ALT
- NOT elevated by exercise — so if GGT is high, it is genuinely liver-related
- Elevated GGT alongside elevated AST/ALT confirms hepatic stress
- Also elevated by alcohol consumption — be honest about intake

BILIRUBIN:
- Normal range: 0.1-1.2 mg/dL
- Bilirubin is a waste product the liver must process and excrete
- Elevated bilirubin = Liver struggling to clear waste
- Mildly elevated (1.2-2.0): May be Gilbert's syndrome (benign genetic condition) — check baseline
- Over 2.0 mg/dL with other liver markers elevated = Significant hepatic dysfunction
- Very high bilirubin causes jaundice (yellowing of eyes and skin) — this is a medical emergency

ALBUMIN:
- Normal range: 3.5-5.0 g/dL
- Albumin is a protein produced by the liver
- Low albumin = Liver is not producing protein properly. This is a very concerning sign of advanced liver damage
- Below 3.5 g/dL alongside elevated liver enzymes = Seek medical evaluation
- Also drops with severe kidney disease (nephrotic syndrome)

--- KIDNEYS ---

BUN (BLOOD UREA NITROGEN):
- Normal range: 7-20 mg/dL
- Can be elevated on high-protein diets — up to 25 mg/dL is generally acceptable for bodybuilders eating 200-300g+ protein daily
- Over 30 mg/dL = Kidney stress. Investigate further
- Dehydration also elevates BUN — ensure you are well-hydrated before blood draw
- Consistently elevated BUN alongside elevated creatinine = Kidney function declining

CREATININE:
- Normal range: 0.7-1.3 mg/dL
- Naturally higher in muscular individuals due to greater muscle mass and creatine turnover
- Up to 1.5 mg/dL may be YOUR normal if you carry significant muscle mass
- Over 1.8 mg/dL = Investigate kidney function regardless of muscle mass
- Trenbolone is particularly harsh on kidneys — monitor creatinine closely on Tren
- Creatine supplementation can also mildly elevate creatinine

eGFR (ESTIMATED GLOMERULAR FILTRATION RATE):
- Normal: >60 mL/min/1.73m2
- 60-90 = Mildly reduced (but may be a calculation artifact in muscular individuals)
- Below 60 = Kidney impairment. Needs medical evaluation
- Below 30 = Serious kidney disease
- CRITICAL NOTE: Standard eGFR formulas (CKD-EPI, MDRD) use creatinine and significantly UNDERESTIMATE kidney function in very muscular people. A 250lb lean bodybuilder may show eGFR of 55 with perfectly healthy kidneys. Cystatin C-based eGFR is MORE ACCURATE for bodybuilders — request this specifically if your standard eGFR is borderline

Paul Barnett on Anabolic Bodybuilding: "I had a doctor tell me my kidneys were failing based on my eGFR. I weighed 280 pounds at the time. Got the cystatin C test done and my kidneys were perfectly fine. The standard formula just does not work for people our size. Every bodybuilder needs to know this."

BUN/CREATININE RATIO:
- Normal: 10:1 to 20:1
- High ratio (>20:1) can indicate dehydration, GI bleeding, or high protein intake
- Low ratio (<10:1) can indicate liver disease or low protein intake
- Use in conjunction with individual BUN and creatinine values for complete picture

--- LIPIDS ---

TOTAL CHOLESTEROL:
- Normal: <200 mg/dL
- On cycle: Will likely be elevated. AAS dysregulate cholesterol metabolism
- 200-240 = Borderline. Manageable with supplements and cardio
- Over 240 = High. Consider lipid-support supplements aggressively

LDL (LOW-DENSITY LIPOPROTEIN — "BAD" CHOLESTEROL):
- Normal: <100 mg/dL
- Optimal: <70 mg/dL
- On cycle: Expect 100-160+ mg/dL. Orals are significantly worse than injectables for LDL elevation
- Trenbolone is the worst offender for lipid destruction among all AAS
- Testosterone alone at moderate doses may only mildly elevate LDL
- Over 160 mg/dL = Cardiovascular risk is significantly elevated. Aggressive intervention needed

HDL (HIGH-DENSITY LIPOPROTEIN — "GOOD" CHOLESTEROL):
- Normal: >40 mg/dL (>60 is ideal)
- AAS CRUSH HDL — this is one of the most consistent and concerning side effects
- On cycle: HDL may drop to 15-30 mg/dL
- Below 20 mg/dL = Very concerning. Your cardiovascular protection is severely compromised
- Management: Citrus bergamot 1000mg/day, 30-45 minutes of cardio 4-5x/week, fish oil 3-4g/day
- HDL recovers relatively quickly off cycle (4-8 weeks) but remains suppressed while on

Dave Crosland on Drugs N Stuff: "HDL is the one that worries me the most with long-term users. You can have a guy on TRT for years and his HDL just sits at 25. That is decades of reduced cardiovascular protection. You need to be doing cardio, taking your citrus bergamot, and watching this number."

TRIGLYCERIDES:
- Normal: <150 mg/dL
- Elevated by poor diet, AAS use, growth hormone, and especially insulin use
- Over 200 mg/dL = Needs dietary and supplemental intervention
- Over 500 mg/dL = Pancreatitis risk. Medical intervention needed
- Fish oil 3-4g/day is the most effective supplement for lowering triglycerides

Lp(a) (LIPOPROTEIN A):
- Genetic marker — largely determined by your DNA
- Not significantly affected by AAS but important to know your baseline
- Elevated Lp(a) = Increased cardiovascular risk that supplements and lifestyle cannot easily fix
- If elevated, you should be extra cautious with compounds that worsen lipids

ApoB (APOLIPOPROTEIN B):
- Better predictor of cardiovascular risk than LDL alone
- Each atherogenic particle (LDL, VLDL, IDL) carries one ApoB molecule
- Normal: <90 mg/dL. Optimal: <60 mg/dL
- Should be tested at baseline and monitored on cycle
- Provides a more complete picture of cardiovascular risk than standard lipid panel alone

--- BLOOD CELLS (CBC) ---

HEMATOCRIT (HCT):
- Normal range: 38-50%
- AAS increase erythropoiesis (red blood cell production), especially Equipoise (Boldenone), high-dose Testosterone, and Anadrol
- Over 50% = Getting elevated. Monitor closely, increase hydration
- Over 52% = Blood is becoming dangerously thick. Clot risk is increasing. Plan to donate blood or get therapeutic phlebotomy
- Over 54% = DONATE BLOOD OR GET THERAPEUTIC PHLEBOTOMY IMMEDIATELY. This is the #1 acute danger marker on cycle. Risk of stroke, pulmonary embolism, and heart attack rises dramatically
- This is not theoretical — elevated hematocrit kills people

Scott McNally on Blood Sweat and Gear: "Hematocrit is the one that can actually kill you acutely. Your cholesterol being bad for 12 weeks is not going to give you a heart attack tomorrow. But a hematocrit of 56% can give you a stroke tonight. Check it, manage it, do not ignore it."

HEMOGLOBIN:
- Normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL
- Correlates directly with hematocrit — elevated together = polycythemia
- Over 18 g/dL = Same concern as elevated hematocrit. Blood is too thick
- Hemoglobin carries oxygen — more is not better when it makes your blood viscous

RBC COUNT (RED BLOOD CELL COUNT):
- Normal range: 4.5-5.5 million/mcL
- Elevated by AAS, especially Equipoise and high-dose testosterone
- Follows the same pattern as hematocrit and hemoglobin
- All three elevated together confirms AAS-induced polycythemia

WBC COUNT (WHITE BLOOD CELL COUNT):
- Normal range: 4,000-11,000/mcL
- Elevated = Possible infection, inflammation, or immune response
- Low = Immune suppression. Can occur post-cycle when the body is stressed
- AAS do not typically cause dramatic WBC changes, but monitor as part of overall health
- Very high WBC (>15,000) with no obvious infection warrants medical investigation

PLATELETS:
- Normal range: 150,000-400,000/mcL
- Low platelets (thrombocytopenia) = Increased bleeding risk
- Very high platelets (thrombocytosis) = Increased clotting risk, especially combined with elevated hematocrit
- AAS do not commonly affect platelets directly but monitor alongside hematocrit for complete clotting risk assessment

--- CARDIAC ---

CRP (C-REACTIVE PROTEIN):
- Normal: <1.0 mg/L
- General inflammation marker — elevated by AAS use, heavy training, illness, or injury
- 1.0-3.0 mg/L = Moderate inflammation
- Over 3.0 mg/L = Significant systemic inflammation. If persistent, this increases cardiovascular risk
- High-sensitivity CRP (hs-CRP) is the preferred test
- AAS are pro-inflammatory — expect some elevation on cycle

HOMOCYSTEINE:
- Normal: <10 umol/L
- Cardiovascular risk marker — elevated homocysteine damages blood vessel walls
- AAS can elevate homocysteine
- Over 15 umol/L = Increased cardiovascular and clotting risk
- Easy to manage: B6 (50-100mg/day), B12 (1000mcg/day), and Folate (800mcg/day) effectively lower homocysteine

BNP or NT-proBNP:
- Cardiac stress and heart failure marker
- Detects early heart enlargement and ventricular strain
- Should be baseline tested before first cycle and monitored annually for long-term users
- Elevated levels indicate the heart is under strain — this is the marker that catches cardiac remodeling early
- If persistently elevated, get an echocardiogram

Dave Palumbo on RXMuscle: "BNP is the one most guys do not know about. If you have been using gear for years, especially with high-dose testosterone and growth hormone, you need to know your BNP. It tells you if your heart is being stressed before you get symptoms."

BLOOD PRESSURE (not bloodwork but CRITICAL):
- Target: <140/90 mmHg (ideally <130/80)
- On cycle: Check DAILY with a home monitor. No exceptions
- If consistently >140/90: Lifestyle interventions (sodium reduction, cardio, potassium intake)
- If consistently >150/90: Medicate. Telmisartan 40-80mg daily (preferred — also has cardioprotective and anti-inflammatory properties) or Lisinopril 10-20mg daily
- Over 180/110 = Hypertensive crisis. Seek immediate medical attention
- AAS, especially Dianabol, Anadrol, and high-dose Testosterone, significantly elevate blood pressure

--- OTHER IMPORTANT MARKERS ---

FASTING GLUCOSE:
- Normal: 70-100 mg/dL
- HGH and MK-677 can significantly raise fasting glucose through insulin resistance
- 100-125 mg/dL = Pre-diabetic territory. Monitor closely, consider reducing GH dose or adding metformin
- Over 126 mg/dL = Diabetic range. Medical intervention needed
- insulin use demands extremely careful glucose monitoring — hyperglycemia and hypoglycemia are both dangerous
- Must be truly fasted (10-12 hours, water only) for accurate reading

HbA1c (GLYCATED HEMOGLOBIN):
- 3-month average of blood sugar control
- Normal: <5.7%
- 5.7-6.4% = Pre-diabetic. If you are using GH or insulin, this needs immediate attention
- Over 6.5% = Diabetic. Discontinue or reduce GH, add metformin, consult physician
- Essential test for anyone using growth hormone, MK-677, or insulin

Skipp Hill on It's Just Bodybuilding: "The guys running high-dose growth hormone for months on end without checking their blood sugar are playing with fire. I have seen guys become legitimately diabetic from GH abuse. Get your HbA1c checked."

PSA (PROSTATE SPECIFIC ANTIGEN):
- Normal: <4.0 ng/mL
- AAS can elevate PSA — testosterone is converted to DHT which stimulates prostate growth
- Get a BASELINE before your very first cycle
- Rising PSA trend over time (even if still under 4.0) = Investigate prostate health
- Over 4.0 ng/mL = Prostate evaluation needed (ultrasound, possible biopsy referral)
- DHT-derived compounds (Masteron, Proviron, Winstrol) may have more prostate impact
- Men over 35 should include PSA in every panel

THYROID (TSH, free T3, free T4):
- TSH normal: 0.5-4.0 mIU/L (optimal 1.0-2.5)
- free T3 normal: 2.3-4.2 pg/mL
- free T4 normal: 0.8-1.8 ng/dL
- AAS can affect thyroid function
- growth hormone can lower T3 — at higher GH doses (4+ IU/day), T3 supplementation (25-50mcg/day) may be needed to prevent hypothyroid symptoms
- Symptoms of low T3: fatigue, cold intolerance, sluggish metabolism, constipation, brain fog
- Trenbolone is known to affect thyroid markers in some users

FERRITIN:
- Normal range: 30-300 ng/mL
- Iron storage marker
- Can get dangerously low from frequent blood donation (done to manage elevated hematocrit)
- Below 30 ng/mL = Iron depleted. Supplement with iron (ferrous bisglycinate 25-50mg/day with vitamin C for absorption)
- Below 15 ng/mL = Severely depleted. You may develop anemia even with elevated hematocrit
- If you are donating blood every 8 weeks to manage HCT, monitor ferritin at every blood draw

VITAMIN D (25-HYDROXYVITAMIN D):
- Optimal: 40-60 ng/mL
- Below 30 ng/mL = Insufficient. Impacts testosterone production, immune function, bone health, and mood
- Supplement: 5000 IU/day for most people. Retest in 8 weeks and adjust
- Crucial for overall hormonal health — vitamin D functions as a hormone itself

IGF-1:
- Reflects growth hormone output
- Age-dependent normal ranges
- Useful for monitoring GH dosing efficacy
- Also indicates liver health (IGF-1 is produced by the liver in response to GH)
- Baseline measurement before starting GH allows you to see the dose-response

=== ENHANCED vs STANDARD RANGES ===

Standard laboratory reference ranges are calibrated for natural, sedentary adults. They are NOT appropriate for interpreting bloodwork in enhanced athletes. You must understand the context of each marker.

MARKERS WHERE "OUT OF RANGE" IS EXPECTED ON CYCLE:
- total Testosterone: Will be far above range (1500-5000+ ng/dL). This is the point of using exogenous testosterone. Not a cause for concern in itself
- free Testosterone: Similarly supraphysiological. Expected
- LH and FSH: Will be suppressed to near zero. This is NORMAL on cycle — exogenous testosterone shuts down the HPTA. Only a concern if they remain zero POST-PCT
- AST: May be 40-80 purely from intense training. Context matters — look at ALT alongside it
- Creatinine: Muscular individuals routinely show 1.2-1.5 mg/dL with perfectly healthy kidneys
- eGFR: Standard formulas UNDERESTIMATE kidney function in muscular people. Cystatin C-based eGFR is the accurate test for bodybuilders
- BUN: High-protein diets (200-300g+/day) naturally elevate BUN to 20-28 mg/dL

MARKERS WHERE "IN RANGE" STILL NEEDS ATTENTION:
- Estradiol at 55 pg/mL: Technically near upper normal range, but for an enhanced male this is getting high. Gyno risk is increasing
- HDL at 35 mg/dL: Technically "low normal" on the lab sheet, but trending down rapidly on cycle means it will keep dropping
- Hematocrit at 50%: Within "normal" range but for someone on EQ and 500mg test, this will climb to 53-55% by cycle end without intervention

MARKERS WHERE THE STANDARD RANGE IS ACCURATE REGARDLESS:
- Liver enzymes (ALT, GGT, bilirubin, albumin): Liver damage is liver damage. These ranges apply to everyone
- Lipids (LDL, HDL, triglycerides): Cardiovascular risk does not care how muscular you are
- Hematocrit: Blood viscosity physics apply equally to everyone. Over 52% is dangerous for anyone
- Fasting glucose and HbA1c: Diabetes does not discriminate based on fitness level
- Blood pressure: Hypertension is hypertension
- CRP, homocysteine, BNP: Inflammatory and cardiac markers apply universally

Scott McNally on Blood Sweat and Gear: "You have to be your own advocate with doctors. Most doctors see your testosterone at 3000 and panic. They see your creatinine at 1.4 and say your kidneys are failing. You need to educate yourself so you can have an informed conversation and know which numbers actually matter."

=== HOW TO ORDER BLOODWORK ===

THROUGH YOUR DOCTOR:
- Tell them you want to monitor your health markers
- You do not have to disclose AAS use, but being honest with a supportive doctor gives you the best care
- Some doctors will order comprehensive panels through insurance

DIRECT-TO-CONSUMER LAB SERVICES (No doctor needed):
- DiscountedLabs.com: Excellent bodybuilder-specific panels at competitive prices
- Marek Health: Founded with the enhanced community in mind. Comprehensive panels with telehealth follow-up
- PrivateMDLabs: Wide selection of individual tests and panels
- LabsMD: Another solid option for self-ordered labs
- These services provide a lab requisition — you take it to Quest Diagnostics or LabCorp for the actual blood draw

COST:
- Basic hormone panel: $50-100
- Comprehensive panel (hormones + liver + kidneys + lipids + CBC): $150-250
- Full enhanced athlete panel (everything above + cardiac markers + thyroid + metabolic): $200-350
- This is less than the cost of one vial of testosterone. Budget for it

PREPARATION FOR ACCURATE RESULTS:
- Fast 10-12 hours before the draw (water only). This is essential for accurate lipid and glucose readings
- Draw at TROUGH levels: Morning, before your next injection. This gives you the lowest point in your dosing cycle and the most useful hormone data
- Avoid heavy training 24-48 hours before the draw. Intense exercise falsely elevates AST, ALT, CK, and can affect other markers
- Avoid alcohol for 48-72 hours before the draw
- Stay well hydrated the day before (dehydration elevates hematocrit, BUN, and other markers artificially)
- Take your regular supplements and medications as normal (unless your doctor says otherwise)
- Note the time of your last injection on your lab order — this helps interpret hormone levels accurately

Dave Palumbo on RXMuscle: "Guys always ask me where to get bloodwork. It has never been easier. You can order everything online, walk into a LabCorp, and have results in 48 hours. The cost is nothing compared to what you spend on gear and food. Make it a non-negotiable part of your budget."

=== RED FLAGS — WHEN TO STOP YOUR CYCLE ===

These values represent immediate danger. If you see ANY of the following, take action NOW — not next week, not after you finish the vial.

STOP ALL ORAL STEROIDS IMMEDIATELY IF:
- ALT over 200 U/L
- AST over 200 U/L with ALT also significantly elevated
- GGT over 200 U/L
- Bilirubin over 2.5 mg/dL with elevated liver enzymes
- Albumin below 3.0 g/dL
- Jaundice (yellow eyes or skin) — this is a medical emergency

DONATE BLOOD / THERAPEUTIC PHLEBOTOMY IMMEDIATELY IF:
- Hematocrit over 54%
- Hemoglobin over 18.5 g/dL
- If you cannot donate (deferred or ineligible), seek therapeutic phlebotomy through a doctor

REDUCE DOSE OR DISCONTINUE CYCLE IF:
- eGFR below 45 (using cystatin C method for accuracy)
- Creatinine over 2.0 mg/dL
- Blood pressure consistently over 160/100 despite medication
- LDL over 200 mg/dL with HDL below 20 mg/dL (catastrophic lipid profile)
- Fasting glucose over 140 mg/dL
- HbA1c over 6.5%
- BNP or NT-proBNP significantly elevated above your baseline
- Resting heart rate consistently over 100 bpm
- PSA doubling or rising above 4.0 ng/mL

SEEK IMMEDIATE MEDICAL ATTENTION IF:
- Chest pain, pressure, or tightness
- Sudden severe headache (could be stroke from elevated HCT)
- Sudden vision changes
- Shortness of breath at rest
- Swelling in one leg (possible DVT — deep vein thrombosis)
- Coughing blood
- Jaundice
- Dark brown/cola-colored urine (rhabdomyolysis or severe liver dysfunction)
- Extreme abdominal pain with nausea (possible liver/kidney crisis)

Dave Crosland on Drugs N Stuff: "I do not care what cycle you are on or what your goals are. If your bloodwork shows red flags, you stop. Full stop. No show, no competition, no amount of gains is worth dying over. I have buried friends in this sport. Do not be another statistic."

Paul Barnett on Anabolic Bodybuilding: "Ego kills in this game. I have seen guys get bloodwork back showing their liver is in crisis and they say they will just add more TUDCA and keep going. That is insanity. Your body is telling you to stop. Listen to it."

=== BLOODWORK FOR WOMEN ===

Female enhanced athletes require different panels with different reference ranges. Virilization markers must be monitored closely.

ESSENTIAL FEMALE PANEL:

total TESTOSTERONE:
- Normal female range: 15-70 ng/dL
- On low-dose Anavar (5-10mg/day): May rise to 80-150 ng/dL
- Over 200 ng/dL = Significant virilization risk. Reduce dose or discontinue
- Women are far more sensitive to androgens — small changes in testosterone produce large effects

free TESTOSTERONE:
- Normal female range: 0.1-6.4 pg/mL
- Even mildly elevated free Testosterone causes androgenic effects in women
- Monitor closely — this is more predictive of virilization than total Testosterone

ESTRADIOL:
- Normal female range: Varies dramatically with menstrual cycle phase
- Follicular phase: 30-120 pg/mL
- Ovulatory peak: 130-370 pg/mL
- Luteal phase: 70-250 pg/mL
- AAS can suppress estradiol in women — low estrogen causes bone density loss, vaginal dryness, mood disruption
- Women should NOT use aromatase inhibitors unless specifically indicated — crashing female estrogen is extremely harmful

SHBG:
- Normal female range: 18-144 nmol/L
- Often significantly elevated in women on oral birth control (can reach 200+ nmol/L)
- High SHBG binds up free Testosterone — may reduce both desired and undesired androgenic effects
- AAS (especially orals) lower SHBG — stopping birth control while on Anavar creates a double drop that can spike free androgens

DHEA-S:
- Normal female range: 35-430 mcg/dL (age-dependent)
- Adrenal androgen marker — elevated levels may indicate adrenal overproduction
- Useful for assessing overall androgenic load in women
- Some women supplement DHEA — this will elevate the reading

PROLACTIN:
- Normal female range: 2-29 ng/mL (higher than males)
- Elevated by 19-nors, stress, and some medications
- Very high prolactin can disrupt menstrual cycle and cause galactorrhea (breast milk production)
- Monitor if using any 19-nor compounds (which women generally should NOT use)

THYROID (TSH, free T3, free T4):
- Same ranges as men apply
- Women are more susceptible to thyroid disruption
- AAS and cutting protocols can suppress T3
- Hypothyroid symptoms in women on gear: extreme fatigue, inability to lose fat despite severe deficit, hair thinning, cold extremities

LH AND FSH:
- Vary with menstrual cycle phase
- AAS will suppress both, potentially causing amenorrhea (loss of menstrual cycle)
- Cycle cessation is common and expected on AAS — but prolonged amenorrhea post-cycle warrants medical evaluation
- Recovery of menstrual cycle post-AAS can take 2-6+ months

LIVER, KIDNEY, LIPID, AND CBC PANELS:
- Same markers and ranges as men
- Women tend to have lower baseline hematocrit (36-44%) — AAS-induced elevation is still a concern
- Lipid disruption from AAS affects women equally

VIRILIZATION MONITORING:
- Bloodwork alone does not catch virilization — you must monitor physical symptoms:
- Voice deepening (often irreversible)
- Clitoral enlargement (often irreversible)
- Facial hair growth
- Acne on jaw, chest, and back
- Male-pattern hair thinning
- At FIRST sign of virilization: STOP the compound immediately. Some effects are permanent

Skipp Hill on It's Just Bodybuilding: "Women need to be especially careful with bloodwork because the margin of error is much smaller. A male can be at double his natural testosterone and be fine. A female at double her natural testosterone is already virilizing. The dose-response curve is completely different."

=== INTERPRETING YOUR RESULTS — PRACTICAL APPROACH ===

Step 1: Compare everything to YOUR baseline. Population averages matter less than your personal trend.

Step 2: Look at markers in context, not isolation. One elevated value may mean nothing. Multiple related markers trending in the same direction tells a story.

Step 3: Trend over time is more important than any single reading. One high AST reading could be from a hard workout. Three consecutive high readings with GGT also elevated = genuine liver problem.

Step 4: Address the worst markers first. If your hematocrit is 55% and your LDL is 140, donate blood today and worry about lipids tomorrow.

Step 5: Adjust your protocol based on data. This is the entire point. If your estradiol is 70, increase your AI. If your prolactin is 25, increase cabergoline. If your liver is struggling, drop the orals. Let the numbers guide your decisions.

Scott McNally on Blood Sweat and Gear: "Bloodwork is not just a checkbox. It is a tool. Every set of labs should inform a decision. Either everything is fine and you continue as planned, or something needs adjusting. Either way, you are making an informed choice instead of a blind one."

=== CONCLUSION ===

Bloodwork is the single most important health practice for any enhanced athlete. It transforms guessing into informed decision-making. It catches problems before they become emergencies. It gives you the data to optimize your protocol for both results and longevity.

The cost is minimal. The inconvenience is minor. The information is invaluable.

Get your baseline. Get your mid-cycle check. Get your post-PCT verification. Monitor your health on cruise. No exceptions.

For personalized bloodwork interpretation, cycle design based on YOUR lab values, and ongoing health monitoring guidance, the coaches at Forged by Freedom (forgedbyfreedom.org) provide expert, data-driven support. Your bloodwork tells the story — Forged by Freedom helps you read it.


=== FILE: Bloodwork Intervention Ladders and Corrective Protocols [CYCLE_GUIDE_17].txt ===

BLOODWORK INTERVENTION LADDERS AND CORRECTIVE PROTOCOLS
Forged by Freedom Cycle Design Guide #17
Source: Precision Bloodwork and Physique Transformation for Enhanced Bodybuilders — Field Manual
Written by Wendy and Bryan Antonelli, Co-Owners and Head Trainers at Forged by Freedom Strength and Nutrition

=== INTRODUCTION ===

When bloodwork comes back out of range, the question isn't IF you should act — it's HOW. This guide provides structured 4-tier intervention ladders for every major body system affected by PEDs. Each ladder escalates from lifestyle modifications through supplements, pharmaceuticals, and finally discontinuation thresholds.

These protocols are for EDUCATIONAL purposes — pharmaceutical interventions should always be discussed with a physician or sports medicine doctor familiar with enhanced athletes.

As Scott McNally (ThinkBig Bodybuilding, Blood Sweat and Gear) emphasizes: "Having bad labs isn't the end of the world. Having bad labs and doing NOTHING about it — that's what ends careers."

Dave Palumbo (RXMuscle) has stated: "Every marker has a fix. The question is whether you're disciplined enough to implement it before things get out of hand."

=== LADDER 1: LIPID RESCUE PROTOCOL ===

Lipid destruction is the #1 cardiovascular risk in enhanced bodybuilding. DHT derivatives, orals, and 19-nors all damage the lipid profile. This ladder addresses HDL collapse, LDL elevation, and ApoB spikes.

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE INTERVENTIONS (First Line, Always):
- Cardiovascular exercise: Zone 2 cardio 150-300 min/week MINIMUM
  * LISS (Low Intensity Steady State): 30-45 min daily at 120-140 BPM
  * HIIT: 1-2 sessions/week (proven to raise HDL)
- Diet modifications:
  * Soluble fiber: 30-40 g/day (oats, psyllium husk, beans, vegetables)
  * Mediterranean-style fats: olive oil, avocado, nuts, fatty fish
  * Reduce saturated fat intake during oral cycles
  * Increase omega-3 rich foods (salmon, sardines, mackerel)
- Eliminate alcohol completely during any cycle
- Maintain healthy body fat (don't get excessively heavy in off-season — visceral fat worsens lipids)

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT INTERVENTIONS:
- Citrus Bergamot: 500-1000mg 2x/day
  * Shown to lower LDL 20-30% and raise HDL in studies
  * Most studied natural lipid support for athletes
- Fish Oil (high EPA/DHA): 2-4g combined EPA/DHA daily
  * Primarily lowers triglycerides
  * Anti-inflammatory (reduces hs-CRP)
- Psyllium Husk: 5-10g/day (soluble fiber — binds bile acids, lowers LDL)
- Plant Sterols/Stanols: 2g/day (can lower LDL 5-15%)
- Niacin (Vitamin B3): 500-1500mg/day (extended release)
  * Raises HDL effectively
  * Can cause flushing — use extended release form
  * Monitor liver enzymes (hepatotoxic at high doses)
- Red Yeast Rice: 1200-2400mg/day (contains natural lovastatin)
  * Basically a low-dose statin — acts the same way
  * Monitor liver enzymes

TIER 3 — PHARMACEUTICAL INTERVENTIONS (Discuss with physician):

STATIN OPTIONS:
- Rosuvastatin (Crestor): 5-20mg/day
  * MOST POTENT LDL/ApoB reduction (up to 60% LDL lowering)
  * Modest HDL increase (5-10%)
  * CAVEAT: May raise HbA1c by 0.3-0.4% — important if running GH/insulin
  * Best for: Pure lipid rescue when LDL/ApoB is dangerously high
  * Start at 5mg, titrate up based on response

- Pitavastatin (Livalo): 2-4mg/day
  * Strong LDL reduction with HDL support
  * A1c NEUTRAL or slightly improved — does NOT worsen glucose
  * IDEAL choice if running GH, insulin, or any compound affecting glucose
  * Best for: Athletes on GH/insulin stacks who need lipid rescue without metabolic penalty
  * Dave Palumbo (RXMuscle): "If you're running GH and your lipids are trashed, pitavastatin is the smarter choice over rosuvastatin because it won't make your glucose worse."

- Ezetimibe (Zetia): 10mg/day
  * Blocks cholesterol absorption in the gut
  * 15-20% additional LDL reduction
  * No A1c effect
  * STACK with any statin for enhanced LDL lowering
  * Best for: Added to statin when statin alone isn't enough

- PCSK9 Inhibitors (Repatha, Praluent): Injectable, every 2-4 weeks
  * Most powerful LDL lowering available (60-70%)
  * Very expensive
  * Reserved for: Genetic predisposition (high Lp(a)) or severe refractory cases

TIER 4 — DISCONTINUATION THRESHOLDS (Pull the Plug):
- HDL <15 mg/dL → Remove offending compound immediately (usually orals/DHTs)
- LDL >200 mg/dL with ApoB >150 → Remove offending compound + start statin
- HDL <20 AND LDL >180 simultaneously → Cardiovascular emergency zone — remove orals, reduce all compounds
- If lipids don't improve after 8 weeks of Tier 3 → End the cycle entirely

LIPID RECOVERY TIMELINE:
- After removing orals: HDL begins recovering in 4-6 weeks
- Full HDL recovery: 8-12 weeks (sometimes longer with repeated abuse)
- LDL normalization with statin: 4-8 weeks
- ApoB response to statin: 4-8 weeks

=== LADDER 2: LIVER PROTECTION PROTOCOL ===

Oral anabolics (C17-aa compounds) are the primary liver stressors. Understanding TRUE liver stress vs training-related enzyme spikes is critical.

KEY DIAGNOSTIC RULE:
- ALT/AST elevated ALONE → May be from heavy training (muscle breakdown releases AST)
- ALT/AST elevated + GGT elevated → TRUE hepatotoxicity (drug-induced liver injury)
- Bilirubin elevated + dark urine → CHOLESTASIS (bile flow blockage) — EMERGENCY

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE INTERVENTIONS:
- Zero alcohol during any oral cycle (non-negotiable)
- Zero acetaminophen (Tylenol) during oral cycles
- Minimize NSAID use (ibuprofen, naproxen — additional liver stress)
- Hydration: 4-5 liters/day minimum
- Avoid excessive fructose (can contribute to fatty liver/NAFLD)
- Keep body fat reasonable (visceral fat → fatty liver)

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT INTERVENTIONS:
- TUDCA (Tauroursodeoxycholic Acid): 500-1000mg/day
  * Gold standard OTC liver support for oral steroid users
  * Protects against bile acid toxicity (cholestasis prevention)
  * Take WITH meals, ideally separate from oral steroid by 2-3 hours
  * 500mg/day for mild orals (Anavar, tbol), 1000mg/day for harsh orals (Superdrol, Anadrol)

- NAC (N-Acetyl Cysteine): 600-1200mg/day
  * Glutathione precursor — the liver's primary antioxidant
  * 600mg for maintenance, 1200mg during oral cycles
  * Used in hospitals for acetaminophen overdose — proven hepatoprotective

- Milk Thistle (Silymarin): 420-600mg/day
  * Modest support — not as effective as TUDCA/NAC but adds mild protection
  * Take standardized extract (80% silymarin)

- Vitamin E: 400-800 IU/day (for NAFLD/fatty liver support)

TIER 3 — PHARMACEUTICAL INTERVENTIONS:
- UDCA/Ursodiol (Prescription): 250-500mg 2x/day
  * Prescription-strength bile acid support
  * Best for cholestasis prevention/treatment
  * More potent than OTC TUDCA
  * Scott McNally (ThinkBig): "If you're going to run harsh orals, UDCA is the real deal. TUDCA is the OTC version, but if your doctor will prescribe Ursodiol, that's the stronger option."

- Dose Reduction of Offending Oral:
  * Cut oral dose by 50% and recheck labs in 3-4 weeks
  * If improvement → continue at reduced dose
  * If no improvement → proceed to Tier 4

TIER 4 — DISCONTINUATION THRESHOLDS:
- ALT/AST >3x Upper Limit of Normal (ULN) + GGT elevated → STOP ALL ORALS immediately
- ALT >200 U/L → Stop orals, seek medical evaluation
- Bilirubin >2.0 mg/dL → Cholestasis risk — STOP immediately, medical evaluation
- Dark urine + jaundice (yellow skin/eyes) + itching → CHOLESTASIS EMERGENCY — stop ALL orals, seek immediate medical care
- If enzymes don't normalize within 6-8 weeks after stopping orals → Medical workup required (ultrasound, hepatology referral)

LIVER RECOVERY TIMELINE:
- After stopping orals: Enzymes begin dropping within 1-2 weeks
- ALT/AST normalization: typically 4-6 weeks
- GGT normalization: 2-12 weeks (variable)
- Bilirubin normalization: 2-4 weeks if caught early
- Full recovery from cholestasis: 6-12 weeks

=== LADDER 3: KIDNEY PROTECTION PROTOCOL ===

Kidney damage is often SILENT until advanced. Muscular athletes face additional challenges because standard kidney markers (creatinine, eGFR) are UNRELIABLE in people with high muscle mass.

CRITICAL DIAGNOSTIC RULES:
- Creatinine elevated in a muscular person → May be FALSE POSITIVE (muscle mass raises baseline)
- eGFR appears low in a muscular person → Standard formulas (CKD-EPI, MDRD) underestimate true kidney function
- ALWAYS use Cystatin-C-based eGFR for accurate assessment in enhanced athletes
- Urinalysis showing protein/microalbumin → TRUE kidney stress signal regardless of muscle mass

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE INTERVENTIONS:
- Hydration: MINIMUM 4-5 liters/day; more if sweating heavily or using diuretics
- Moderate sodium intake: Don't eliminate sodium (electrolyte balance matters), but don't overdo it
- Protein management: 1.0-1.5g/lb is fine for healthy kidneys; reduce toward 1.0g/lb if kidney stress detected
- Blood pressure control: THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT KIDNEY PROTECTOR
  * Monitor BP daily with home cuff
  * Target: <125/80 mmHg
- Avoid NSAIDs: Ibuprofen, naproxen crush renal blood flow — especially dangerous during prep or dehydration
- Avoid contest diuretic abuse: Acute renal shutdown from aggressive diuretic use kills bodybuilders

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT/LIFESTYLE ESCALATION:
- Increase cardio specifically for BP management
- Potassium-rich foods (if not on ACE/ARB): bananas, sweet potatoes, spinach
- Magnesium: 400-800mg/day (glycinate or citrate form)
- CoQ10: 200-400mg/day (renal protective antioxidant)
- Reduce 19-nor doses if kidney markers are trending up

TIER 3 — PHARMACEUTICAL INTERVENTIONS:
- Telmisartan (ARB): 40-80mg/day
  * GOLD STANDARD kidney-protective medication for enhanced athletes
  * Lowers blood pressure
  * Provides DIRECT renal protection beyond BP lowering (reduces proteinuria, preserves GFR)
  * Improves insulin sensitivity (bonus benefit)
  * Big Paul (Anabolic Bodybuilding): "Telmisartan is the bodybuilder's blood pressure medication. It protects the kidneys, it helps with insulin sensitivity, and it keeps your BP in check. If you're running heavy compounds, talk to your doctor about it."

- Lisinopril (ACE Inhibitor): 10-20mg/day
  * Alternative to telmisartan if ARB not available/tolerated
  * Also renal protective
  * Can cause dry cough (common ACE side effect)
  * Do NOT combine ACE + ARB (hyperkalemia risk)

- GLP-1/GIP Agonists (Semaglutide, Tirzepatide, Retatrutide):
  * Lower A1c and triglycerides → reduce "diabetic-like" kidney vascular strain
  * Indirect kidney protection via metabolic improvement
  * Especially valuable for GH + insulin users

- Compound Modification:
  * Reduce or remove 19-nors (especially Tren — nephrotoxic)
  * Reduce testosterone dose if BP contribution is significant
  * Remove EQ if contributing to polycythemia → thicker blood → kidney strain

TIER 4 — DISCONTINUATION THRESHOLDS:
- eGFR <60 (confirmed by Cystatin-C) → Medical evaluation, reduce/stop nephrotoxic compounds
- eGFR <45 → SERIOUS kidney impairment — STOP all compounds except TRT, nephrology referral
- Protein in urinalysis (proteinuria) → Remove 19-nors immediately, add telmisartan
- Creatinine >2.0 mg/dL (confirmed not muscle-mass artifact via Cystatin-C) → Stop cycle, medical evaluation
- Any signs of acute kidney injury (sudden creatinine spike, oliguria, edema) → EMERGENCY — stop everything, seek immediate medical care

KIDNEY RECOVERY NOTES:
- Acute kidney stress from dehydration/diuretics: Usually recovers with rehydration (days to weeks)
- Chronic kidney decline from BP/19-nors: May be PARTIALLY reversible if caught early
- Permanent GFR loss: DOES happen with chronic abuse — this damage is IRREVERSIBLE
- You cannot replace kidneys. Treat them as your most valuable asset.

=== LADDER 4: HEMATOLOGY PROTOCOL (Hematocrit/Hemoglobin) ===

Elevated hematocrit (thick blood) increases risk of stroke, heart attack, and blood clots. Testosterone, EQ, and Anadrol are the primary drivers.

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE INTERVENTIONS:
- Hydration: 4-5+ liters/day (dehydration falsely elevates HCT and increases viscosity)
- Naringin (grapefruit extract): 500-1000mg/day
  * May modestly reduce hematocrit
  * Also supports lipid metabolism
- Cardiovascular exercise: 150+ min/week (improves blood flow, reduces viscosity)
- Aspirin: LOW dose 81mg/day (antiplatelet — discuss with physician)
  * Reduces clot formation risk but does NOT lower HCT
  * Only a band-aid, not a fix

TIER 2 — BLOOD DONATION / PHLEBOTOMY:
- Therapeutic Phlebotomy (Blood Donation): Most direct fix
  * Donate 1 unit (approximately 500mL) when HCT exceeds 52%
  * Can donate every 56 days (Red Cross standard)
  * Immediate HCT reduction of 2-3%
  * CAUTION: Frequent donation → iron deficiency, fatigue, lower performance
  * Monitor ferritin alongside HCT — if ferritin drops <30 ng/mL, supplement iron or space donations

- Therapeutic Phlebotomy (Physician-Ordered):
  * Can be done more frequently than blood bank donations
  * Physician can order based on HCT level regardless of donation eligibility
  * More controlled approach for chronic elevation

TIER 3 — COMPOUND MODIFICATION:
- Reduce testosterone dose (lower dose = less erythropoietic drive)
- Remove EQ/Boldenone (strongest HCT-elevating injectable)
- Remove Anadrol (strong EPO stimulator)
- Switch to lower-dose TRT if on blast
- Telmisartan 40-80mg may modestly reduce hematocrit drive (indirect effect)

TIER 4 — EMERGENCY THRESHOLDS:
- HCT >54% → IMMEDIATE phlebotomy + compound reduction
- HCT >56% → MEDICAL EMERGENCY — risk of stroke/PE/DVT, stop cycle, seek medical care
- Hemoglobin >18.5 g/dL → Parallel emergency threshold
- Symptoms of hyperviscosity (headache, vision changes, dizziness, chest pain) → EMERGENCY regardless of HCT level

=== LADDER 5: BLOOD PRESSURE PROTOCOL ===

High blood pressure is the silent killer in bodybuilding. Water retention, thick blood, vasoconstriction from Tren, and kidney strain all contribute.

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE INTERVENTIONS:
- Sodium management: Don't eliminate — BALANCE sodium with potassium and magnesium
  * 2,000-3,000mg sodium/day (not zero)
  * 3,500-4,700mg potassium/day (from food: sweet potatoes, bananas, spinach)
  * 400-800mg magnesium/day (glycinate or citrate)
- Cardiovascular exercise: 150-300 min/week zone 2 cardio
- Reduce caffeine/stimulant intake (especially pre-workouts with high stim content)
- Improve sleep quality: Sleep apnea is a SILENT BP driver in large bodybuilders
  * If snoring/gasping at night → get a sleep study
  * CPAP treatment can drop BP 10-15 mmHg
- Stress management: Chronic stress → elevated cortisol → elevated BP
- Maintain healthy body weight: Excessive off-season mass raises BP independently

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT INTERVENTIONS:
- Potassium supplementation (if dietary intake insufficient): 500-1000mg/day
- Magnesium: 400-800mg/day (vasodilator effect)
- Beet root powder/juice: 500mg or 8oz juice daily (nitric oxide → vasodilation)
- CoQ10: 200-400mg/day
- Garlic extract (aged): 600-1200mg/day (modest BP reduction)

TIER 3 — PHARMACEUTICAL INTERVENTIONS:

- Telmisartan (ARB): 40-80mg/day — FIRST-LINE CHOICE
  * Gold standard for bodybuilders
  * BP reduction + kidney protection + insulin sensitivity improvement
  * Start at 40mg, increase to 80mg if needed
  * Well-tolerated, no significant performance impact

- Lisinopril (ACE Inhibitor): 10-20mg/day — Alternative first-line
  * Effective BP control + kidney protection
  * Can cause dry cough (switch to ARB if bothersome)
  * Do NOT combine with ARB

- Nebivolol (Beta-Blocker): 5-10mg/day
  * Cardio-protective, lowers heart rate
  * Better performance preservation than older beta-blockers (atenolol, metoprolol)
  * Nitric oxide enhancement (unique among betas)
  * Good for: Athletes with elevated resting HR + BP

- Amlodipine (Calcium Channel Blocker): 5-10mg/day
  * Potent vasodilator
  * Reserved for stubborn cases not controlled by ARB alone
  * Can cause ankle edema
  * Can combine with ARB for dual therapy

- Combination Therapy: ARB + CCB (telmisartan + amlodipine) for resistant hypertension

TIER 4 — EMERGENCY THRESHOLDS:
- BP consistently >140/90 → Start pharmaceutical intervention (don't wait)
- BP >160/100 → URGENT — start medication immediately + reduce compounds
- BP >180/110 → HYPERTENSIVE CRISIS — seek immediate medical care
- Symptoms: Severe headache, vision changes, chest pain, nosebleeds with high BP → EMERGENCY

=== LADDER 6: CARDIAC MARKER PROTOCOL ===

Beyond lipids and BP, cardiac-specific markers detect deeper cardiovascular damage.

TIER 1 — MONITORING:
- hs-CRP (high-sensitivity C-Reactive Protein):
  * Measures systemic inflammation
  * <1.0 mg/L = low risk
  * 1.0-3.0 mg/L = moderate risk
  * >3.0 mg/L = high risk — investigate and intervene
  * Can be elevated by training — retest after 48hr rest if suspicious

- Homocysteine:
  * Elevated homocysteine = independent cardiovascular risk factor
  * Normal: <10 umol/L
  * Elevated: >12 umol/L
  * High risk: >15 umol/L

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT INTERVENTIONS:
- For elevated hs-CRP:
  * Fish oil 2-4g EPA/DHA daily (anti-inflammatory)
  * Curcumin 500-1000mg/day (with piperine for absorption)
  * Reduce training volume temporarily if CRP consistently elevated

- For elevated Homocysteine:
  * Methylated B-Vitamins:
    - Methylfolate (5-MTHF): 800mcg-1mg/day
    - Methylcobalamin (B12): 1000-2000mcg/day
    - P5P (active B6): 50-100mg/day
  * TMG (Trimethylglycine/Betaine): 1000-3000mg/day
  * These typically normalize homocysteine within 4-8 weeks

TIER 3 — ADVANCED CARDIAC MONITORING:
- BNP / NT-proBNP:
  * Brain natriuretic peptide — marker of cardiac strain/heart failure
  * Normal: <100 pg/mL (BNP) or <125 pg/mL (NT-proBNP)
  * Elevated = heart is under strain → cardiology referral
  * Can be mildly elevated post-intense training — retest at rest

- Troponin:
  * Marker of heart muscle damage
  * Should be UNDETECTABLE in healthy individuals
  * ANY persistent elevation → cardiology referral IMMEDIATELY
  * Can be transiently elevated after extreme exercise — retest at rest

TIER 4 — IMAGING/SPECIALIST TRIGGERS:
- Echocardiogram recommended if:
  * BNP/NT-proBNP persistently elevated
  * >5 years of heavy PED use
  * LVH (Left Ventricular Hypertrophy) symptoms: shortness of breath, exercise intolerance
  * Family history of cardiac disease
  * Any troponin elevation

- CIMT (Carotid Intima-Media Thickness): Measures arterial wall thickness — early atherosclerosis detection
- Coronary Calcium Score (CAC): CT scan measuring calcium in coronary arteries — hard atherosclerosis
- Both recommended as baseline after age 35 for long-term enhanced athletes

=== LADDER 7: METABOLIC/GLUCOSE PROTOCOL ===

GH, insulin, and 19-nors can all impair glucose metabolism. This ladder addresses rising fasting glucose, A1c drift, and insulin resistance.

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE INTERVENTIONS:
- Cardiovascular exercise: 150-300 min/week (PROVEN to improve insulin sensitivity via GLUT4 upregulation)
- Carb timing: Focus carbohydrates around training (peri-workout) rather than spreading evenly
- Fiber intake: 30-40g/day (slows glucose absorption)
- Sleep: 7-9 hours (sleep deprivation worsens insulin resistance significantly)
- Body composition: Reduce visceral fat (even small reductions improve insulin sensitivity dramatically)

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT INTERVENTIONS:
- Berberine: 500mg 2-3x/day (with meals)
  * Works similarly to metformin (AMPK activation)
  * Can lower fasting glucose 15-30 mg/dL
  * Can lower A1c 0.3-0.5%
  * OTC alternative to metformin
  * Scott McNally (ThinkBig): "Berberine is the poor man's metformin. It works. If your glucose is creeping up on GH, try berberine before you ask your doctor for a prescription."

- ALA (Alpha-Lipoic Acid): 300-600mg/day
  * Improves glucose uptake
  * Antioxidant properties

- Chromium Picolinate: 200-1000mcg/day
  * Modest insulin sensitivity improvement
  * Works best in those who are chromium-deficient

- Cinnamon Extract (Ceylon): 1000-2000mg/day
  * Modest glucose-lowering effect

TIER 3 — PHARMACEUTICAL INTERVENTIONS:

- Metformin: 500-2000mg/day (titrate up slowly)
  * Gold standard for insulin resistance
  * Lowers hepatic glucose output
  * Improves insulin sensitivity
  * Start 500mg with dinner → increase to 500mg 2x/day → max 2000mg/day
  * GI side effects (diarrhea, nausea) — use extended release (XR) to minimize
  * Monitor B12 levels (metformin can deplete B12 long-term)

- GLP-1 Agonists:
  * Semaglutide: 0.25 → 0.5 → 1.0 → 2.4mg weekly (titrate every 4 weeks)
  * Tirzepatide: 2.5 → 5.0 → 7.5 → 10 → 12.5 → 15mg weekly
  * Both POWERFULLY lower A1c (1-2%), improve insulin sensitivity, and suppress appetite
  * Ideal for GH + insulin users who need metabolic protection

- GH Dose Reduction:
  * If A1c >5.7 on GH → reduce GH by 25-50%
  * If A1c >6.0 on GH → reduce GH significantly or add metformin/GLP-1

TIER 4 — DISCONTINUATION THRESHOLDS:
- Fasting glucose consistently >126 mg/dL → Diabetic range — STOP insulin use, reduce GH, start metformin, medical evaluation
- A1c >6.5% → Diabetic — STOP all glucose-impairing compounds, full medical workup
- C-peptide declining while on exogenous insulin → Beta-cell burnout — STOP insulin use immediately
- Symptoms of severe insulin resistance: Acanthosis nigricans, extreme fatigue, rapid fat gain despite diet control → Full metabolic panel, endocrinology referral

=== LADDER 8: ESTROGEN MANAGEMENT PROTOCOL ===

Estrogen (E2) management is one of the most common and most frequently botched aspects of PED use.

CRITICAL PRINCIPLE: Estrogen is NOT the enemy. It is essential for joint health, libido, mood, cardiovascular protection, and muscle growth. The goal is BALANCE, not elimination.

SIGNS OF high E2:
- Water retention / bloating
- Gynecomastia (nipple sensitivity, lump behind nipple)
- Mood swings / emotional volatility
- Blood pressure elevation
- Decreased libido (paradoxical in some)

SIGNS OF crashed E2 (from excessive AI use):
- Joint pain / cracking joints
- Depression / flat mood / anhedonia
- Poor libido / erectile dysfunction
- Dry skin
- Poor muscle gains (estrogen is anabolic)
- Fatigue

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE / DOSE MANAGEMENT:
- Reduce testosterone dose (less substrate = less aromatization)
- Increase injection frequency (more frequent = more stable levels = less E2 spikes)
  * Example: Instead of 500mg 1x/week → 250mg 2x/week or 170mg 3x/week
- Body fat management: Higher body fat = more aromatase enzyme = more E2
- DIM (Diindolylmethane): 200-400mg/day — mild estrogen metabolizer (modest effect)

TIER 2 — AI DOSING LADDER (Use ONLY when symptomatic + labs confirm high E2):

Anastrozole (arimidex):
- Starting dose: 0.25mg every other day (EOD) or 0.5mg 2x/week
- Moderate dose: 0.5mg EOD
- High dose: 1mg EOD (RARELY needed — high risk of crashing E2)
- Mechanism: Reversible aromatase inhibitor (E2 can rebound if stopped suddenly)
- Skipp Hill (ThinkBig): "Start LOW with your AI. 0.25mg EOD. Wait a week, pull labs, see where you're at. Most guys crash their E2 by going too aggressive too fast."

Exemestane (Aromasin):
- Starting dose: 6.25mg EOD or 12.5mg 2x/week
- Moderate dose: 12.5mg EOD
- High dose: 25mg EOD (AGGRESSIVE — only for very high aromatizers)
- Mechanism: Suicidal/irreversible aromatase inhibitor (no rebound, but harder to reverse if you overshoot)
- Take with fat (improves absorption by 40%)
- Slightly beneficial for lipids vs anastrozole

TIER 3 — SERM FOR GYNO PREVENTION/TREATMENT:
- Tamoxifen (Nolvadex): 10-20mg/day
  * Blocks estrogen at the breast receptor
  * Does NOT lower circulating E2 — blocks its effect at tissue level
  * First-line for active gyno flare
  * Can use alongside AI or alone

- Raloxifene: 60mg/day
  * Superior to tamoxifen for gyno reduction (studies show better reversal of existing tissue)
  * Less systemic effects than tamoxifen
  * Harder to source

TIER 4 — crashed E2 RECOVERY:
If E2 is crashed (<10 pg/mL):
- STOP all AIs immediately
- STOP any anti-estrogenic compounds (Masteron, Proviron — they mask E2)
- Wait 1-2 weeks for anastrozole to clear (reversible)
- Exemestane crash takes LONGER to recover (suicidal inhibitor — 2-3 weeks for new aromatase enzyme production)
- May temporarily increase testosterone dose to provide more aromatization substrate
- dbol (Dianabol) 10-20mg/day for 5-7 days: Aromatizes heavily, can rescue crashed E2 quickly (advanced technique)
- Dave Palumbo (RXMuscle): "crashed estrogen is worse than high estrogen. high E2 you can fix with a little AI. crashed E2 makes you feel like death and there's no instant fix — you just have to wait for it to come back."

=== LADDER 9: PROLACTIN MANAGEMENT PROTOCOL ===

Elevated prolactin is primarily caused by 19-nor compounds (Nandrolone, Trenbolone, MENT) via progesterone receptor activation and dopamine suppression.

SYMPTOMS OF HIGH PROLACTIN:
- Sexual dysfunction (ED, low libido, anorgasmia)
- Gynecomastia (prolactin + E2 together = highest gyno risk)
- Mood instability / depression
- Lactation (rare but possible — "leaky nipples")

TIER 1 — LIFESTYLE / PREVENTION:
- Manage E2 properly (high E2 + high prolactin = synergistic gyno risk)
- Adequate sleep (prolactin naturally rises with sleep deprivation)
- Reduce stress (cortisol-prolactin axis)
- Zinc: 25-50mg/day (modest prolactin suppression)
- Vitamin E: 400 IU/day

TIER 2 — SUPPLEMENT INTERVENTION:
- P5P (Pyridoxal-5-Phosphate, active B6): 100-200mg/day
  * Increases dopamine activity → lowers prolactin
  * First-line supplement approach
  * May be sufficient for mild prolactin elevation (20-30 ng/mL)
  * Dave Crosland (ThinkBig, Drugs N Stuff): "P5P at 100-200mg a day handles prolactin for most guys on moderate Deca doses. You don't need to jump straight to caber."

- Vitamin B6: 50-100mg/day (less effective than P5P but still helpful)
- Mucuna Pruriens: 300-500mg/day (contains L-DOPA, a dopamine precursor)

TIER 3 — PHARMACEUTICAL INTERVENTION:
- Cabergoline (Dostinex): 0.25-0.5mg 2x/week
  * MOST EFFECTIVE prolactin-lowering drug
  * Dopamine agonist (D2 receptor)
  * Start at 0.25mg 2x/week, increase if needed
  * Typically brings prolactin to normal within 2-4 weeks
  * CAUTION: Cardiac valve issues with chronic high-dose use (monitor echocardiogram if used >6 months)
  * Do NOT use "preventatively" — only when prolactin is confirmed elevated + symptomatic
  * Big Paul (Anabolic Bodybuilding): "Caber works like magic for prolactin, but it's not candy. Use it when you need it, confirm with bloodwork, and get off it when you can."

- Pramipexole (Mirapex): 0.125-0.5mg/day
  * Alternative dopamine agonist
  * Used when cabergoline isn't available or tolerated
  * Can cause nausea, dizziness
  * Similar cardiac valve concerns at high doses long-term

TIER 4 — COMPOUND MODIFICATION:
- Reduce 19-nor dose (primary cause of prolactin elevation)
- Switch from Deca to NPP (shorter ester = faster clearance if problems arise)
- Remove Tren if prolactin is uncontrollable despite cabergoline
- Remove MENT immediately if prolactin + E2 are both severely elevated (double-hit compound)
- Prolactin >50 ng/mL → Rule out prolactinoma (pituitary tumor) — MRI recommended

=== LADDER 10: THYROID MANAGEMENT PROTOCOL ===

GH use, contest prep caloric restriction, and chronic stress can all suppress thyroid function.

TIER 1 — MONITORING:
- TSH: 0.5-2.5 mIU/L (optimal athletic range — standard labs say <4.5 but that's too broad)
- Free T3: 3.0-4.0 pg/mL (the ACTIVE thyroid hormone)
- Free T4: 1.0-1.5 ng/dL
- Reverse T3: <15 ng/dL (elevated rT3 = thyroid conversion issue, common in caloric deficit)

TIER 2 — GH-INDUCED T3 SUPPRESSION:
- GH suppresses T4 → T3 conversion
- Monitor Free T3 every 8-12 weeks on GH
- If Free T3 dropping below 2.5 pg/mL → supplementation needed

TIER 3 — INTERVENTION:
- Cytomel (T3): 12.5-25mcg/day
  * Replaces GH-suppressed T3
  * Start at 12.5mcg, increase to 25mcg if needed
  * Do NOT exceed 50mcg/day (catabolic at high doses)
  * Always taper off (don't stop abruptly)

- For contest prep thyroid suppression:
  * Same T3 supplementation approach
  * Reverse T3 elevation in deficit → add T3 12.5-25mcg

TIER 4 — FULL THYROID RECOVERY (post-cycle/post-prep):
- Stop exogenous T3
- TSH may be suppressed for 4-8 weeks
- Full thyroid recovery: typically 6-12 weeks
- If TSH doesn't recover after 12 weeks → endocrinology referral

=== QUICK REFERENCE: RED FLAG THRESHOLDS DEMANDING IMMEDIATE ACTION ===

MARKER                  | RED FLAG LEVEL           | IMMEDIATE ACTION
Hematocrit              | >54%                     | Phlebotomy + reduce compounds
Hemoglobin              | >18.5 g/dL               | Same as HCT protocol
ALT                     | >200 U/L                 | Stop all orals, medical eval
ALT + GGT both elevated | >3x ULN                  | Stop orals immediately
Bilirubin               | >2.0 + symptoms           | CHOLESTASIS — ER
eGFR (Cystatin-C based) | <45                      | Stop cycle, nephrology
HDL                     | <15 mg/dL                | Remove offending compounds
LDL + ApoB              | >200 + >150              | Statin + compound removal
Blood Pressure          | >180/110                 | Hypertensive crisis — ER
Fasting Glucose         | >126 mg/dL consistently  | Diabetic range — medical eval
HbA1c                   | >6.5%                    | Diabetic — stop all glucose-impairing compounds
Prolactin               | >50 ng/mL                | Rule out prolactinoma — MRI
Troponin                | Any persistent elevation  | Cardiology referral IMMEDIATELY
BNP/NT-proBNP           | >300 pg/mL at rest       | Cardiology referral

=== THE BOTTOM LINE ===

Every out-of-range marker has a solution. The intervention ladder gives you a systematic approach:
1. Lifestyle first (always)
2. Supplements second (specific, evidence-based, with doses)
3. Pharmaceuticals third (with physician guidance, specific drugs and doses)
4. Pull the plug when thresholds are crossed (non-negotiable)

As Wendy and Bryan Antonelli write in the Precision Bloodwork Field Manual: "The enhanced path is not safe. It's not simple. But with precision, ownership, and discipline, it can be sustainable."

The athletes who last decades are the ones who follow the ladders. The ones who skip steps are the ones who end up in the ER — or worse.


=== FILE: Complete Female Steroid and PED Protocols [CYCLE_GUIDE_12].txt ===

COMPLETE FEMALE STEROID AND PED PROTOCOLS
Expert Sources: Scott McNally (ThinkBig), Dave Crosland (ThinkBig), Skipp Hill (ThinkBig), Dave Palumbo (RXMuscle), Paul Barnett (Anabolic Bodybuilding)
Reference Guide for Forged by Freedom AI Coach

=== WHY FEMALE PROTOCOLS ARE FUNDAMENTALLY DIFFERENT ===

Women are 10-20x more sensitive to androgens than men. What constitutes a "low dose" for a man is often a massive dose for a woman. Compound SELECTION matters more than dose — some compounds should NEVER be used by women regardless of dose. Virilization can be permanent and irreversible.

Scott McNally: "Ladies, stick with Anavar and maybe low-dose Primo. That's all you need. The women who get into trouble are the ones who try to use male compounds at 'lower doses' — it doesn't work that way. Your receptors are far more sensitive."

Paul Barnett on Anabolic Bodybuilding: "For women, Anavar is it. It's the one compound I feel comfortable recommending. Anything else and you're playing with fire. I've seen women permanently damage their voices with one cycle of Winstrol."

Dave Palumbo on RXMuscle: "Female athletes need to understand that their margin for error is tiny compared to men. One bad compound choice can have consequences that last the rest of your life."

=== COMPOUNDS SAFE FOR WOMEN ===

ANAVAR (Oxandrolone) — THE GOLD STANDARD FEMALE COMPOUND:

Anavar is the safest, most well-studied steroid for female use. It has an anabolic rating of 322-630 but an androgenic rating of only 24 (testosterone is 100/100). It does not aromatize, has minimal liver toxicity, and its short half-life (9 hours) means it clears fast if sides appear.

Beginner Female (first cycle):
- Dose: 5mg/day
- Duration: 6 weeks
- No testosterone base needed
- Support: NAC 600mg/day for liver
- Expected results: Noticeable strength gains (10-15% on major lifts), mild muscle hardening, some fat loss, improved recovery
- Monitor weekly: voice, skin, body hair

Intermediate Female (2-3 cycles completed):
- Dose: 10mg/day (split 5mg AM / 5mg PM for stable blood levels)
- Duration: 6-8 weeks
- Optional addition: Testosterone Propionate 5-10mg/week OR Primobolan 25-50mg/week
- Support: NAC 600mg/day, fish oil 3g/day
- Expected results: Visible muscle development, significant strength gains, improved body composition, muscle fullness

Advanced Female (4+ cycles, competitive):
- Dose: 15-20mg/day (this is considered HIGH for women)
- Duration: 6-8 weeks maximum
- May stack with Primobolan 50-75mg/week
- Support: NAC 600mg/day, TUDCA 250mg/day
- Bloodwork every 4 weeks
- Virilization monitoring: WEEKLY voice recordings, photo documentation
- Expected results: Competition-level conditioning, significant lean mass, strength comparable to male beginners

PRIMOBOLAN (Methenolone Enanthate — injectable):

The second-best option for women. Very mild, no aromatization, quality lean gains. The main downside is its longer ester (half-life 7-10 days), which means if virilization starts, the compound takes longer to clear than Anavar.

Beginner Female: 25-50mg/week (one injection per week)
Intermediate Female: 50-75mg/week
Advanced Female: 75-100mg/week

Notes:
- Verify sourcEQuality — Primo is the most counterfeited steroid (often substituted with Masteron, which is much more androgenic)
- Janoshik testing can verify compound identity
- PIP (post-injection pain) can be significant at high concentrations — seek 100mg/mL formulations
- Can be run for longer cycles (12-16 weeks) due to mild profile
- Stack with Anavar for the ultimate female competition cycle

TESTOSTERONE (micro-dose):

Small doses of testosterone can dramatically improve femalEQuality of life — libido, energy, mood, recovery, and mild anabolic effect.

Beginner Female: Not recommended as first compound. Start with Anavar.
Intermediate Female: 5mg/week (Testosterone Propionate preferred)
Advanced Female: 5-15mg/week

Notes:
- MUST use Testosterone Propionate (half-life ~0.8 days) — if virilization appears, it clears in 2-3 days
- Do NOT use Testosterone Enanthate or Cypionate — their lonGHalf-lives (4-5+ days) mean weeks of exposure if sides hit
- Testosterone cream/gel at 1-5mg/day is an alternative for HRT-level benefits
- Many female TRT protocols use 5-10mg/week successfully for years
- Monitor free Testosterone, total Testosterone, and SHBG on bloodwork

NANDROLONE / NPP (very low dose — advanced only):

Nandrolone provides excellent joint support and mild mass building. Women must ONLY use NPP (Nandrolone Phenylpropionate) — never Deca (Nandrolone Decanoate).

Advanced Female Only: 25-50mg/week
- NPP half-life: 2.5-3 days (clears relatively fast)
- Deca half-life: 6-12 days — TOO LONG for women. If virilization starts, you're stuck with it for weeks.
- Even at 25mg/week, monitor for virilization
- Prolactin monitoring needed even at low female doses
- Provides exceptional joint relief for women with joint issues from training

=== COMPOUNDS WOMEN MUST NEVER USE ===

These compounds will cause virilization at ANY dose. There is no "safe female dose" for these compounds. The risk is not worth the reward when safer alternatives exist.

ANADROL (Oxymetholone): NEVER.
Despite some outdated medical literature citing low-dose Anadrol (1-2mg/kg) in women for anemia treatment, real-world virilization is rapid and severe. Voice deepening can occur within 2-3 weeks and is often permanent. The androgenic:anabolic ratio on paper looks mild (45:320) but the real-world androgenic effects in women are extreme due to receptor sensitivity.

DIANABOL (Methandrostenolone): NEVER.
Highly androgenic in practice, aromatizes heavily (estrogen management nightmare for women), causes rapid virilization. Voice changes, facial hair, body hair — often within the first 2 weeks. Water retention masks the virilization signs until it's too late.

TRENBOLONE (any ester — Acetate, Enanthate, Hex): NEVER.
The most androgenic steroid available (androgenic rating: 500). Will virilize at ANY dose. Beyond physical virilization, Trenbolone causes severe mental sides in women — anxiety, insomnia, aggression, paranoia. There is absolutely no reason for any woman to use Trenbolone when Anavar and Primobolan exist.

WINSTROL (Stanozolol): NEVER.
Despite its undeserved reputation as a "mild cutting drug," Winstrol is devastatingly androgenic for women. Voice changes and clitoral enlargement occur rapidly, often within 2-3 weeks. Many women have reported permanent voice damage from aromasinGLE Winstrol cycle. The DHT-derivative structure makes it particularly harsh on female androgen receptors.

HALOTESTIN (Fluoxymesterone): NEVER.
Extremely androgenic (androgenic rating: 1,900). Used by male powerlifters for pre-competition aggression. Extremely hepatotoxic. No place in female protocols whatsoever.

SUPERDROL (Methasterone): NEVER.
Extreme androgenic potency despite marketing as "mild." Severe liver toxicity on top of virilization risk.

METHYLTRIENOLONE (M-Tren / oral tren): NEVER.
